# run9 英語レビュー: ワールド33・34（レッスン 961〜1020）

- 日付: 2026-11-27
- やりかた: docs/run9/BRIEF.md の「Review method」。lint → 3人の レビュアー（A=アメリカの 小学校・中学校の 先生 / B=こどもの ESL・言語習得の 専門家 / C=台本を 読む 声優）を べつべつに 書いて から 決める。2人 いじょうが 指摘 or 客観的な まちがい（objective）だけ なおす。10 レッスンずつ 6 グループで レビュー
- lint（`npm run english:lint -- --from 961 --to 1020`）: **まえ error 71 / warn 49 / info 14 → あと error 60 / warn 47 / info 17**（のこりは ぜんぶ わざと: L1011〜1018 の イギリスと アメリカを くらべる フォニックスの ことば〈colour・centre・theatre・favourite・queue・timetable・pavement・biscuit・rubbish〉と その せつめい・スライド〈テストと w34Phonics.ts の 表で きまって いる〉、L1013 の イギリスから きた ブラウンさんの「on holiday」「biscuits」、タイトル「Make Sure You…」「Would You Mind…?」「Do You Know Where…?」「Could You Recommend…?」と その スライド〈テスト〉、L977 の「osahashimo」〈日本の 学校の ことばと して しょうかい〉。info は 会話の「What is」・「very, very」・くんれんの ほうそうの「Please listen carefully.」・「Let's」4回〈AI の「Let's pretend」の ため〉）
- なおした 欄: **456**（パッチ 480 件。ブロックの きめごと 116・AI の さいしょの ひとこと 60・レビュアー 284・あとの そろえ 20）。なおした レッスン: **60 / 60**
- 60 レッスン ぜんぶに `"reviewed": "run9"` を つけた
- パッチは ぜんぶ「before が いまの 値と おなじ ときだけ あてる」で、HEAD から じゅんばんに あてなおした（はずれ 0）。60 レッスン ぜんぶで スライドの 検証・台本の 長さ（180〜240 秒）・英語の わりあい・かいわ あいて（partner.ts）・文法の じゅんばん（order.mjs。テストが 見ない teacher.segments も）を たしかめた（ALL OK）

## 共通の きめごと（ブロック全体）
1. イギリスふうの ほめことば: 「Brilliant!」→「Great job!」（L962・L971・L986・L1005・L1013）・「Fantastic!」（L994）・「Great job, email writers!」（L1016）、「Lovely ～!」→「Wonderful work! / Nice job! / Wonderful journals!」（L982・L996・L1018。L982 会話の Lovely は グループ）
2. エマ先生 じぶんの 語り: 「Ms. Emma says / asks / practices / reads」→「I say / I ask / I give a practice announcement / I read the first question」、「Ms. Emma has a secret / got her suitcase / Ms. Emma's passport」→ I / my、日本語「エマ先生「」→「わたし「」（L966〜970・L1002・L1005〜1010）。teacherScript の「Ms. Emma says / takes us / her pouch」も L991〜1000（グループ）と L1001〜1020（20-after）で I / my / me に。ほかの 人の カード・スカイの ことば・メールの しょめいは なまえの まま
3. 日本語の 先生の なまえ「ミス・エマ」→「エマ先生」（W34 だけ ちがって いた）、「ミスター・リー」→「リーさん」
4. エマ先生の あいさつ「Good morning / Good evening, ～!」→「Hi / Hello, ～!」（L973・L980・L982・L992・L1001・L1003・L1009・L1011・L1013・L1018・L1019 の さいしょ）。物語の なかの Tonight や、トモおばあちゃん・かかりの 人・リーさん・ほうそうの Good morning / Good evening は のこした
5. L988「Maybe, I promise ～」→「You could say, ～」、L967 autumn → fall
6. **AI の さいしょの ひとこと**: アプリは あいてを こどもの キャラ（ハル・リコ・キコ・ビリモ）から えらび、その かおと なまえで ひとことを 出す。なのに「I'm Ember.」「I'm Dr. Mori.」「I'm Mr. Lee.」「I'm your check-in clerk today.」など べつの 人として 話して いた。goalJa の やくは のこし、60 レッスン ぜんぶ「Let's pretend I'm Ember / Dr. Mori / Grandma Tomo / Skye / Mr. Lee / Mrs. Brown from the UK / a traveler ～.」「Let's pretend I work at ～.」に した（W25-32 と おなじ）。2つめの Let's は「We can / Time to」に、Ding, dong → Ding-dong、Look out of → Look out
7. **イギリスと アメリカ（W34）**: L1011〜1014 は イギリスと アメリカの ちがいを ならう 回。イギリスの ことばは「In the UK, ～」と くらべる とき・フォニックスの ことば・イギリスから きた ブラウンさんの ことばだけ のこし、ブライトウォーター（ドルを つかう まち＝イギリスではない）の かんばん・メニュー・エマ先生と こどもたちの ことばは アメリカ英語に（check・cookie・Science Center・Color Room・Little Theater・Old Theater・sidewalk・subway・line・schedule・elevator）

## 学習項目が かわった もの（イギリスと アメリカの きめごとの ため。テスト・src は かえて いない＝どこにも 書かれて いない）
- L1012 school.expressions[2]「Could we have the bill, please?」→「Could we have the check, please?」（grammar・会話・targetExpressions・goalJa・recap・台本・スライドも）
- L1014 school.expressions[2]「～ It's in the Science Centre, on the second floor.」→「～ Science Center ～」
- L1013 tpr[0]「Press the button for the lift!」→「Press the button for the elevator!」
- L1004 phonics.sounds[2]「/ɡ/」→「/ɡdʒ/」（suggest の gg。ことばは そのまま）
- core 表現・ことば・フォニックスの ことば・タイトルは かえて いない

## のこした こと（1人だけの 指摘・テストで きまって いる・あとで 決める）
- タイトルと スライドの「…」、会話の「…」、L977「osahashimo」
- L1011〜1018 の イギリスの ことば・つづり（フォニックスの 表と テストで きまって いる。「In the UK」と くらべる かたちで だけ 出る）
- L997 core「What's the purpose of your visit? Sightseeing.」（テストの 正規表現で きまって いる）
- TPR「Stay right next to Ms. Emma!」（先生が こどもに いう ふつうの ことば）
- たんどくの 語根・接尾辞（rupt・ject・vis・ize）。ならう 音そのものなので のこした。TTS で きいて たしかめたい
- L991 と w34Phonics.ts の「2けたずつ よむ」（3けたの 便名では みぎから かぞえないと わかりにくい。src の ため のこした）

## レビュアーの メモ（グループごと・英語）

### Run 9 review notes: L1001-1010 (world 34, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, エマ先生, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 23 patches in 7 lessons; L1003, L1006 and L1009 have no change. No school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics word changes. The only learning-adjacent field is L1004 phonics.sounds[2] ("/ɡ/" -> "/ɡdʒ/" for suggest). It is not referenced in tests/ or src/ (w34Phonics.ts `suggest` has no sound field). The grammar order is clean: "would you mind" starts in L1002, "could you tell me where/how/when" in L1003, "recommend" in L1004, "I'm afraid" in L1007, "instead" in L1008 and "that's very kind of you" in L1010. No new text adds an order-pinned form. Themes: (1) **Story contradiction across lessons**: L1004 calls Mr. Lee's recommended restaurant "the Little Anchor", open 5-9 pm. L1011-1012 go to the same place ("Mr. Lee recommended it") as the "River Fish Restaurant" and eat lunch there. L1004's teacherScript now uses that name and lunch-friendly hours. (2) **Wording that misleads**: L1001 seg0 "Last time ... Lesson one thousand!" makes it sound like today is lesson 1000. L1002 quiz option "Yes, I mind." is not English (the trap answer is "Yes, I do."). L1005 "Here you are ... Of course, please bring it back" put "Of course" after the handover. L1004 "It means give an idea". (3) **Spelling / vocab**: "repair man" (US: repairman) -> the L1007 vocab word "repair person" (L1007 teacherScript, L1008 conversation and seg0). (4) **TTS stress cues**: "FOR, ty, one" (L1005) and "THIR, ty" (L1010) -> hyphen beat form. (5) **Phonics fact**: suggest's gg is /ɡdʒ/ in US English, not /ɡ/. Checked and right: room 512 on the fifth floor, then room 803 on the eighth floor. Breakfast is 7-10. Five guests (one adult, four children). Flight 1120 from Skyport matches L998 (gate 41C, 4:20). Clock Tower Station is a five-minute walk. Children's umbrellas are yellow. The suitcase arrives "that night" and is back in L1007. The passport is at the airport Baggage Service desk (L1006 form). The embassy and police advice is for adults and children stay with a grown-up. -ize rules are right (apology / summary / energy / category / memory / sympathy drop the y). The check script passes with ALL OK. All touched lessons are 204-220 s with 100% English, every slide item is valid, and partner.ts is OK.

#### L1001 I Have a Reservation
- A: Mr. Lee, the wooden seagull, the welcome card and "Captain Pancake" are warm and natural. seg2 "The table where hotel staff help you" (a desk is more a counter; single flag, kept).
- B: "under the name" and "one night is one sleep" are clear. The double-letter tips (a-cc-o-mm-o-dation, a-dd-re-ss, ne-c-e-ss-ary) are right. seg0 "Last time, we landed in Brightwater. Lesson one thousand!" says the milestone right after "Last time" with no verb, so a child hears that today is lesson 1000. The JA is the same.
- C: Same seg0 flag: the line sounds like a new announcement. "o, dation" reads fine. Room numbers are written as words in say ("five twelve").
- Decision: seg0 (say, subtitleEn) -> "That was lesson one thousand!" with JA あれが 1000かいめの レッスン だったね！ (A+B). The teacherScript's third-person "Ms. Emma takes us..." is kept (see block-wide notes).

#### L1002 Would You Mind…?
- A: Room 512, the stuffy room, Birimo hugging the only extra pillow and "would you mind sharing?" are natural. seg17 option A "Yes, I mind." is not something a speaker says.
- B: Would you mind + ing, Mind = bothered, "Not at all" = happy to help are well explained, and the allowed JA hint is there. The quiz distractor should be real English: the classic trap is "Yes, I do." (= I don't want to). Coffee "two e's" is still a double letter (kept).
- C: "o, c c, u, p, ied" reads acceptably ("ied" comes out like "eyed", which matches the sound). "Shake your head and say, Not at all!" works with the meaning.
- Decision: seg17 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "A, Yes, I mind." -> "A, Yes, I do." (A+B).

#### L1003 Do You Know Where…?
- A: The map card, the bakery joke and "Stay close to me!" are natural. seg19 "Great walking today!" is a playful nod to the TPR walking (single flag, kept).
- B: The embedded question (is moves to the end) and the A/B quiz are right. "Could you tell me when the next train ... leaves?" is a correct extra model, and L1003 is the start lesson for "could you tell me". The map card directions match the expression (left out of the entrance, two blocks, right at the bakery).
- C: "The word is moves to the end" needs stress on "is", but the slide shows it and it reads OK (single flag, kept).
- Decision: no change.

#### L1004 Could You Recommend…?
- A: Birimo's thunderstorm tummy and the city guide page are fun. seg10 "It means give an idea." drops "to".
- B: Recommend = "This one is good. Try it!" is clear. Story contradiction: here the restaurant is "the Little Anchor", open "from five to nine in the evening", and "We decide to go there another day". L1011 ("we will go to the fish restaurant by the river and order lunch") and L1012 ("Mr. Lee recommended it, remember?", menu header "River Fish Restaurant. Today's Lunch") show the same place with a different name, open at lunch. Phonics: suggest's gg is /ɡdʒ/ in US English (səg-JEST or sə-JEST), but phonics.sounds says /ɡ/.
- C: "r e, c, o, m m, e n d" reads fine. "It means give an idea" sounds clipped.
- Decision: teacherScript: "the Little Anchor" -> "the River Fish Restaurant", reading header -> "River Fish Restaurant.", hours -> "from eleven in the morning to nine at night", and the answer -> "At eleven in the morning." (objective, story). The quoted reading stays well over 200 chars. seg4 "Open at five, close at nine" is a general shop example and is kept. seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "It means to give an idea." (A+B). phonics.sounds[2] "/ɡ/" -> "/ɡdʒ/" (objective, fact).

#### L1005 Hotel Role-Play
- A: The rainy-day sign (blue big umbrellas, yellow for children) and Skye shaking rain from her wings are lovely. Conversation line 4 "Here you are, a yellow one. Of course, please bring it back ..." says "Of course" after handing it over. Birimo's "Welcome back" when they are about to go out is a pretend greeting (single flag, kept).
- B: Borrow vs lend is explained well, and the quiz ("Could you borrow me" vs "lend me") is the right trap. Same line-4 flag: the target "Of course. Please bring it back ..." is split in an odd order. The Gate 41C forty/fourteen stress rule is right.
- C: "FOR, ty, one" makes TTS say a capital "FOR" and then a bare "ty", which can come out spelled.
- Decision: conversation line 4 -> "Of course. Here you are, a yellow one. Please bring it back before you check out." with JA もちろんです。 added, and the seg7 slide item updated (A+B). seg7 say keeps its shorter retelling. seg10 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "FOR, ty, one." -> "FOR-ty-one." (B+C).

#### L1006 My Suitcase Didn't Arrive
- A: Ms. Emma's secret and Haru's "That's why you wore the same green sweater every day!" are charming. The Baggage Service form is realistic.
- B: The rule (stay calm, say the problem, give color, size and marks) and the child-safety line ("stay with your grown-up and ask the staff") are good. -ize rules are right (apology -> apologize, summary -> summarize, real + ize). Flight 1120 from Skyport matches L998.
- C: "Flight eleven twenty" is written as words. "add ize" is read by letter rules like "size" without the s (/aɪz/), which is the sound being taught (kept; see block-wide notes).
- Decision: no change.

#### L1007 I'm Afraid It's Broken
- A: The "oven" room, the rattling air conditioner and Birimo holding the flashlight are natural. The teacherScript says "Sam the repair man". "Cool job, everyone!" is a pun on the air conditioner (single flag, kept).
- B: I'm afraid = polite bad news, not scared, is clear, and the allowed JA hint is there. "repair man" is not a US spelling (repairman is one word), and this lesson's vocab word is "repair person". modernize / energize (drop the y) / finalize are right.
- C: Reads well. "room five twelve" is written as words in say.
- Decision: teacherScript "Sam the repair man" -> "Sam the repair person" (objective).

#### L1008 Could You Change It Instead?
- A: The room-change card (Room 803, eighth floor, gold tower lights, fruit basket) and Birimo asking to ride the cart "instead" are fun. The cold-soup quiz (a different soup instead) is fine for practice.
- B: Instead = in place of, at the end, is clear. Same "repair man" spelling in conversation line 0 and seg0. category -> categorize (drop the y), special / personal + ize are right.
- C: "Sam, the repair man, looked and looked" reads well, but keep the spelling the same as L1007.
- Decision: conversation line 0 and seg0 (say, subtitleEn) "repair man" -> "repair person" (objective). JA しゅうりの サムさん already fits.

#### L1009 I Lost My Passport
- A: Ms. Emma searching her backpack, jacket and suitcase, and the calm "I'm the adult, so I'll ask at the front desk", are good models.
- B: Safety is right: tell an adult, children keep passports with a trusted grown-up, adults go to the police and the embassy. The passport being at the Baggage Service desk ties back to L1006's form. memory / sympathy (drop the y) and symbol + ize are right.
- C: Reads well. "safe and sound" is easy to say.
- Decision: no change.

#### L1010 Problem Solved!
- A: Kiko's thank-you card ("You always smile, even when there is a problem.") is warm. "Love, Kiko, ..." is how kids sign cards. "Thank you for finding my passport" to Mr. Lee is a little generous (he called the airport), but it is a learning expression and fine as thanks (single flag, kept).
- B: Thank you for + noun / ing is right. The review items are right (luggage gg = /ɡ/, vision from vis, 12:30 = twelve thirty, not thirteen).
- C: "THIR, ty." has a capital syllable and a bare "ty" that TTS can spell out.
- Decision: seg10 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "THIR, ty." -> "THIR-ty." (B+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **teacherScript still narrates Ms. Emma in the third person** ("Ms. Emma takes us...", "Ms. Emma says, ...", "She says calmly") in every lesson here (L1001-1010). The block-wide fix only changed say / subtitles. I kept it because the teacherScript is not read by TTS and the brief limits the fix to say / subtitleEn / subtitleJa. If you want full consistency, apply the same "I say / I" swap to teacherScripts across W33-34 as a central pass.
- **Stress beats written as "FOR, ty" / "THIR, ty"**: probably also in the other number lessons (L991-995 Flight / Seat / Gate reviews, L1015, L1020). Grep say for `\b[A-Z]{3,5}, [a-z]{1,4}\b` and use the hyphen form (FOR-ty, THIR-ty, fif-TEEN).
- **Restaurant name**: if any later lesson (L1011-1020) still says "Little Anchor" or gives evening-only hours for the river restaurant, align it with "River Fish Restaurant".
- **"repair man"**: a grep of world-34.json finds it only in L1007-1008, and these patches cover all of it. "Little Anchor" is only in L1004 (patched).
- **Bare "ize" / "vis" in say** (L1006-1010 "add ize", "Ize again", "Vis means see"): kept. Letter rules read "ize" as /aɪz/, which is the target sound. "vis" may come out /vɪs/, a single C flag. Spelling them out (i, z, e) would lose the sound.
- Kept on purpose: "…" in the L1002, L1003, L1004 titles and slides; "Good evening / Good morning" said by Mr. Lee and the kids inside the story; L1003 "Great walking today!"; L1005 "Welcome back"; L1007 "Cool job, everyone!"; L1001 seg2 "table"; L1008 cold-soup quiz; L1010 "Thank you for finding my passport" and "Love, ..." on the card; "Please take your room key", "Please bring it back" (hotel staff to guests); "fish and chips" (common in the US too).

### Run 9 review notes: L1011-1020 (world 34, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 94 patches in all 10 lessons; no lesson is left with no change. Most of the patches are the UK/US policy in L1011-1014. **Three learning items change because the policy requires it**: L1012 school.expressions[2].en "Could we have the bill, please?" -> "Could we have the check, please?", L1014 school.expressions[2].en "... It's in the Science Centre, on the second floor." -> "Science Center", and L1013 tpr[0].en "Press the button for the lift!" -> "Press the button for the elevator!". In each case I also changed every field that has the same text (grammar, conversation, aiTask target, goalJa, recap, slides, say/subtitleEn). No test or src line pins any of these (grep of tests/ and src/ for "bill, please", "Science Cent", "button for the lift": no hits). The core expressions (expressions[0]) are not touched. No phonics word changes. The grammar order is clean: no new text adds "would you mind", "recommend", "I'm afraid", "instead", "I'm writing to" or other order-pinned forms. Themes: (1) **UK/US policy**: British forms kept only as the UK side of a comparison, as the phonics word, or in Mrs. Brown's words. Brightwater signs, menus and brochures (L1011 square sign, L1012 menu, L1014 price list, L1017 brochure) and Ms. Emma's own words (L1012 the check, L1016 email "lovely holiday", L1018 "favourite part") now use US forms. (2) **Story timeline**: L1013 put the hotel meeting "this evening", but L1014 (the museum, "today") and L1015 (Clock Tower, boat, lunch and museum on one day) come after it. I moved it to the afternoon. (3) **TTS cues**: comma-split capital syllables ("EL, e, va, tor", "Va, CA, tion", "Fif, TEEN", "Eigh, TEEN") now use the hyphen beat form, and the bare "ize" in "Final / Modern / Real plus ize" is now "i, z, e" (as the -ize reviews already say). (4) **Small fixes**: JA ミスター・リー -> リーさん, the L1015 JA こんばんは under "Hello", praise said twice in a row, L1015 "Read two numbers at a time" for 760, L1019 keychain "toy". The checker passes with ALL OK. All lessons are 208-225 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L1011 Exploring the City
- A: The walk, the seagull statue and the kind woman with the camera are natural. "Could you is soft and polite" is fine. The Clock Tower Square sign in Brightwater says "stay on the pavement", "The underground station", "join the queue", "check the timetable". A sign in a US-English dollar city would not say that.
- B: The sign is not a UK comparison, not the phonics word slot, and not Mrs. Brown, so the policy calls for US words. The phonics part right after it still teaches underground / pavement / queue / timetable as the UK side, so the kids hear both. The Q, u, e, u, e = "Q" tip is right. Starter and partner (Kiko) are fine.
- C: Reads well. "Bong, bong!" and "Click!" are fine. "Q, u, e, u, e" is read as letters (intended).
- Decision: teacherScript sign -> "Please stay on the sidewalk ... The subway station is across the bridge. Please wait in line at the ticket machines and check the schedule on the wall."; question -> "Where is the subway station?" (objective, US policy). The teacherScript calls Ms. Emma "Ms. Emma" in the third person, as every W34 teacherScript does (not read by TTS; kept).

#### L1012 At the Restaurant
- A: The river window table, the menu prices and "One is enough, Birimo!" are natural. Ms. Emma asking for "the bill" and a Brightwater menu with "a free biscuit" sound British. Kiko asks "Does it come with a biscuit?" while the JA says クッキー.
- B: The brief names this case: expression[2] "Could we have the bill, please?" -> check, in every field. The quiz answer seg19 "The bill comes at the end. In the US, it's the check." flips to "The check comes at the end. In the UK, it's the bill." The phonics part (Bill / In the US, people say check) stays, because bill is the phonics word. Menu math is right (fish soup $8; children's plate $6). Fish and chips is a dish name (kept).
- C: "Bis, kit. Don't say the u!" reads fine. "Super job!" / "Yummy work" fine.
- Decision: check in grammar, expressions[2], conversation[6], target[2], goalJa, recap[1], seg1/7/16/18/19/20 slides, seg7/16/18/20 say+subtitleEn, teacherScript; seg19 say/subtitleEn/JA flipped to the UK side; menu "a free cookie" (twice) and Kiko "Does it come with a cookie?" (all objective, US policy). Learning-item change: expressions[2].en (no test pins it).

#### L1013 Lift or Elevator?
- A: Mrs. Brown and Mr. Green, "You mean the elevator!" and Birimo's "up box" are fun and natural. Mrs. Brown's card is UK English on purpose (kept). The TPR "Press the button for the lift!" is Ms. Emma's own command (JA エレベーター). seg19 ends "Great job!" and seg20 starts "Great job!". "Holiday is from the UK" sounds like the word came from the UK.
- B: The TPR is not a comparison and not Mrs. Brown, so the policy calls for elevator. "Holiday is from the UK" also hides that US English says holiday for special days; "the UK word" matches the JA (イギリスの ことば). Timeline: "This evening, we come back to the Seagull Hotel" / seg0 "Tonight", but L1014 visits the museum "today" and L1015 tells Skye about one day: Clock Tower, boat, lunch, museum. So the hotel meeting cannot be in the evening before the museum. Afternoon tea in the lobby fits.
- C: "EL, e, va, tor" and "Va, CA, tion": TTS may spell out "EL" / "CA" and stops at every comma. "Clap on EL!" has the same problem. "Great job! ... Great job!" sounds repetitive.
- Decision: tpr[0].en and its slides (seg12, seg13) and seg12 say/subtitleEn -> "Press the button for the elevator!" (objective, US policy; learning-item change, no test pins it). teacherScript "After lunch, we stop by the Seagull Hotel."; seg0 "This afternoon, ..." / JA きょうの ごごは (objective, story). seg9 -> "EL-e-va-tor. ... Clap on the first part!" with JA さいしょで (B+C). seg10 -> "Holiday is the UK word. Vacation is the US word. Va-CA-tion." (A+B+C). seg20 "Great job!" -> "Well done!" (A+C; JA already よく できました).

#### L1014 Museum Tickets
- A: The ticket desk, "Friends of all kinds are welcome, and you count as a child!" and the family ticket are natural. The Brightwater price list says "Colour Room", "Science Centre", "Little Theatre". Expression[2] "It's in the Science Centre" uses the UK spelling in a learning sentence.
- B: The brief names this case: Science Centre -> Science Center everywhere it is the expression. The price list is a Brightwater sign -> US spellings. Ticket math is right: 1 x $12 + 4 x $6 = $36 > family $30, so "cheaper" is true. Birimo counts as a child (4 children: Haru, Riko, Kiko, Birimo). teacherScript phonics "Theatre. It's the same!" can be heard as "the spelling is the same" (it means the same r, e pattern as centre).
- C: Reads well. "Tiptoe, tiptoe." is fine. "c, o, l, o, r" is read as letters (intended).
- Decision: Science Center in expressions[2], conversation[4], target[2], recap[1], seg1/7/15/19 slides, seg7/15 say+subtitleEn (objective, US policy; learning-item change, no test pins it); price list -> "Color Room ... Science Center ... Little Theater" (objective, US policy); "It's the same pattern!" (A+B). The phonics part still teaches colour / centre / theatre / favourite as the UK side.

#### L1015 A Day in the City
- A: Skye's notebook and the four-step ladder are lovely. seg8 has Ms. Emma say "Here is your fifth stamp!" as if she gives it; in the story Skye gives it (teacherScript, and Birimo playing Skye in the conversation).
- B: seg0 EN is "Hello, storytellers!" (Hello opening) but the JA still says こんばんは. seg10 / teacherScript "Read two numbers at a time" for Flight 760: 760 is read seven / sixty (one digit, then two digits), and "numbers" means digits here. The order words and past forms are well taught. The day order (Clock Tower -> boat -> lunch -> museum) agrees with Skye's notebook.
- C: "Flight seven sixty" is written as words for TTS (good). "s's" reads fine.
- Decision: seg0 JA -> こんにちは (objective JA). seg8 -> "Skye gives us the fifth stamp. Stamp!" with JA スカイが ... くれたよ (A+B; slide item stays, it is the conversation line). seg10 and teacherScript -> "Read it in two parts." with JA 2つに わけて よもう (A+B). tipJa "2けたずつ よむ" is the block's flight-number rule (L991, w34Phonics.ts); I kept it (see block-wide notes).

#### L1016 An Email to the Hotel
- A: The email to the front desk, "Our room number is enough" and Mr. Lee's quick reply are good models. Ms. Emma's own email says "Thank you for a lovely holiday at the Seagull Hotel." That is British (lovely, holiday), and you thank a hotel for a "stay". "Best wishes," is used in US email too (kept; testPoints name it).
- B: The email is Ms. Emma's own writing, so the policy calls for US English; the phonics part teaches holiday as the UK word right after. Times agree with L1018/L1019 (bus 7:15, flight 10:30, Saturday morning). JA ミスター・リー appears three times (block rule: リーさん). The private-info lesson ("No, we don't need to") is good; goalJa keeps ほんとうの.
- C: "Final plus ize": TTS reads the bare "ize" badly. "That's me!" joke kept.
- Decision: email -> "Thank you for a wonderful stay at the Seagull Hotel." (objective, US policy). ミスター・リー -> リーさん in conversation[6].ja, goalJa, seg7 JA (objective JA). seg10 -> "Final plus i, z, e." (B+C).

#### L1017 Travel Brochure
- A: The three tours, the vote and the snack promise are natural. The Brightwater brochure says "the Old Theatre" and "Children must join with an adult." ("join with" is not natural; "come with" is). seg19 "Excellent!" is followed by "Excellent choosing!" in seg20.
- B: The brief names "the theatre was modernized" -> theater; the same place appears three times. Tour times are right (Castle Walk about two hours is the longest; boat one hour; market ninety minutes). "We already took a boat yesterday" fits the timeline (city day = yesterday, today = last full day). JA ミスター・リー in seg0.
- C: "Modern plus ize" (bare "ize"). "Excellent! / Excellent choosing!" repeats.
- Decision: teacherScript "Old Theater" (3 times) and "Children must come with an adult." (objective, US policy; the wording fix is A+B). seg0 JA リーさん (objective JA). seg10 -> "Modern plus i, z, e." (B+C). seg19 "Excellent!" -> "You got it!" with JA せいかい！ (A+C).

#### L1018 My Travel Journal
- A: Riko's journal, Kiko's ticket and Birimo's one line are warm and natural. Ms. Emma says "then write your favourite part" (UK spelling in her own words). seg19 "Wonderful ears!" is followed by "Wonderful journals!" (block-wide praise) in seg20.
- B: Facts and timeline: market on the first day (L1005), museum on the second day (the city day), bus at 7:15 tomorrow; all agree. "I was surprised by" as a feeling is explained well. Kiko's home-address question repeats L1016, which reinforces the safety point (kept).
- C: "Fif, TEEN! ... FIF, ty." stops inside the word; the hyphen beat form reads better. "Real plus ize" (bare "ize").
- Decision: teacherScript "favorite part" (objective, US policy). seg9 -> "Fif-TEEN! ... FIF-ty." (B+C). seg19 -> "Fif-TEEN. Great listening!" with JA よく きけたね！ (A+B+C). seg10 -> "Real plus i, z, e." (B+C).

#### L1019 Airport of Wings Review
- A: The busy airport, the two requests in a row and the souvenirs are natural. seg5 "Keychain. A little toy that holds your keys." A keychain is not a toy. seg8 "Would you mind means, is it OK for you?": the natural form is "OK with you".
- B: The departures board agrees with L1016 (Flight 318 at ten thirty; bus at 7:15; "two hours before"). "Not at all" = yes, OK is taught clearly, and the quiz distractor "Yes, I mind" is fine. "toy" gives the wrong meaning for keychain. JA ミスター・リー in seg0.
- C: "Eigh, TEEN!" stops inside the word.
- Decision: seg5 -> "A little ring that holds your keys." with JA ちいさな わっか (A+B). seg8 -> "is it OK with you?" (A+B). seg9 -> "Eigh-TEEN!" (B+C). seg0 JA リーさん (objective JA). seg0 JA やあ under "Hello" is the block-wide choice (kept).

#### L1020 World 34 Grand Review
- A: The talk show, Skye's letter and the stamp picture of the Hall of Wisdom are a good finale. Kiko's air-conditioner line matches L1007/L1008 ("Could you give us a different room instead?"). The speed TPR is the pinned "Look! Stop! Come here! Go!".
- B: visible -> visibility, recommend (one c, two m's), memorize and vacation (US) are right. The letter question "Who should come with the friends? Their teacher." is fine.
- C: seg10 "Va, CA, tion." has the same problem as L1013.
- Decision: seg10 -> "Va-CA-tion." (B+C). Ms. Emma voicing Skye's "Congratulations! Here is your sixth stamp!" in conversation[4] is set up with "Skye has something for you" (kept).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Cross-lesson contradiction outside my group: L1004 vs L1012.** In L1004 Mr. Lee recommends "the Little Anchor", a fish restaurant by the river. The brief and L1012 (teacherScript "Mr. Lee recommended it, remember?", menu header, starter "welcome to the River Fish Restaurant") call it the River Fish Restaurant. I kept L1012 (the brief names River Fish Restaurant); L1004 should rename the Little Anchor, or the L1004 group lead should decide.
- **L1008 has "Lift"** (seen in a scan of W34 non-UK lessons); check whether it is a UK comparison. If not, the policy calls for elevator.
- **Flight-number rule "2けたずつ くぎる"** (L991 testPoints/tipJa, w34Phonics.ts `flight`, L999/L1015 tipJa): it is true when you count from the right (7 | 60), but a child may read 760 as "76 | 0". The EN in L1015 is now "Read it in two parts". Consider adding "うしろから" to the JA once in L991 if the lead wants it; I did not touch src or other groups.
- **Comma-split stress cues and bare "ize"** probably recur in L1006-1010 and L991-999 reviews ("Eigh, TEEN", "plus ize", "va, CA, tion"). Grep say for `[A-Z]{2,}, [a-z]` / `, [A-Z]{2,}` and `plus ize`.
- **Back-to-back praise after the block-wide swaps**: "Great job!" next to "Great job!" (L1013 seg19/20), "Wonderful ears / Wonderful journals" (L1018). Check the answer segment against the wrap-up in other lessons that got "Great job!" / "Wonderful".
- **teacherScript third-person "Ms. Emma"** is the house style in all 30 W34 teacherScripts (the narrator is "we"); kept.
- Kept on purpose: Mrs. Brown's UK card and lines (lift, holiday, biscuits); all phonics UK forms and their slides/tips/testPoints; "Best wishes,"; "Fish and chips"; L1011 three bongs; L1016 "That's me!"; L1019 JA やあ under "Hello"; L1020 Ms. Emma reading Skye's stamp line in the conversation; "…" in titles; story-internal "Tonight" (L1015, L1018).

### Run 9 review notes: L961-970 (world 33, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters, fall). There are 45 patches, and every lesson has at least one. There are no learning-item changes: school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en and phonics words are untouched, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is clean. No new text adds make sure / hold on / stay calm / as soon as / unless / rebuilt, and the safety word list is respected. Themes: (1) **Bare letter groups that TTS misreads**: "Add ion" (L961, L963), "Add ly" and "u r e" (L964), "Add ance" / "Keep ance" (L966), "ent / ence / ance" (L967), "Ency sounds like en see" (L968), "Add ty" (L969). They are now spelled as letters (the teacherScripts already did this) or carried by a real word. (2) **Facts and safety**: the L967 typhoon card said only "It is calm there" about the eye. It now adds "but the storm comes back soon", so a child does not go outside in the eye. L966 "fix tall furniture to the wall" means "repair" to a US child, so it is now "attach". (3) **Fragments left by the block-wide I-say swap**: L969 "I practice." and L970 "I read." (4) **Small fixes**: the L961 answer had no speaker; the L963 "When, then a comma" rule was wrong; the L969 chime is now "Ding-dong" everywhere (as the starter has it); L970 "there was no absence"; JA "あつくて あつい" (L962) and "ほんの すこしの センチメートル" (L965). All facts checked are right: four layers, a thin crust like apple skin, a thick hot mantle, the inner core about as hot as the sun's surface, magma vs lava, Hawaii made by volcanoes, geothermal energy, plates moving a few cm a year like fingernails, four plates and about 111 active volcanoes in Japan, typhoons forming over warm water in summer and fall, tsunami as a series of waves that can travel as fast as a jet in deep ocean (go at once, never to the beach, stay until the warning is over), and 119 for fire / ambulance. The check script passes with ALL OK. Lessons run 213-226 s with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L961 The Island of Fire
- A: Harbor Town, the salamander ranger in a yellow helmet and the harbor sign are warm and natural. "That tree is a cherry tree" is a good Your turn. seg8 "Riko asks, When was the last eruption? Its last eruption was..." gives no speaker for the answer, so it sounds like Riko answering herself.
- B: Using that for far things and just = only is clear, and the rupt explanation (e = out) is right. The 300-year eruption fact is the same everywhere (sign, conversation, phonics, L970).
- C: Same seg8 flag (the voice cannot show the speaker change). In seg11, "Add ion" is read as the word ion (eye-on). "Add e d" right after it is spelled out.
- Decision: in seg8 (say, subtitleEn), add "Ember says," before the answer, with JA エンバー「…」 (A+C). seg11 -> "Add i, o, n for the thing." (B+C).

#### L962 Inside the Earth
- A: The cut-open model, Dr. Mori's intro and Haru's "Oops, sorry for interrupting!" are natural and tie in well with interrupt.
- B: The layers are right (thin crust, thick hot mantle, outer core, inner core about as hot as the sun's surface). The JA of seg4 is "あつくて あつい そう", which in hiragana reads as "hot and hot". The EN is "thick, hot" (厚い + 熱い).
- C: Reads well. "Inter plus rupt" is easy to say.
- Decision: seg4 subtitleJa -> "ぶあつくて、とても あつい そう" (objective JA).

#### L963 When a Volcano Erupts
- A: The baking soda volcano card is a clear procedure, and "Keep your safety glasses on" is a good safety touch. In seg8, "When, then a comma, then what happens" is hard to follow.
- B: Magma underground vs lava outside is taught well, three times. seg8's rule puts the comma right after when, but the first clause comes first. The JA has the same gap.
- C: seg8 is choppy. In seg10, "Add ion" is the same eye-on problem as L961.
- Decision: seg8 (say, subtitleEn) -> "When plus one thing, then a comma, then what happens next." with JA to match (B+C). seg10 -> "Add i, o, n." (B+C).

#### L964 Hot Springs and Islands
- A: The stone pool and Ember's tail getting too warm (abrupt / abruptly) are fun. In seg17, "It's sunny, so I go to the park." would be more natural as "I'm going", but it is fine as a model (single flag, kept).
- B: The facts are right (heated underground, old ash makes rich soil, Hawaii made by volcanoes, geothermal electricity). The so = result point is clear.
- C: In seg10, "Add ly" (lee / lie) and "Rupt plus ure" are bare letter groups that TTS misreads.
- Decision: seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Add l, y." and "Rupt plus u, r, e is rupture" (B+C).

#### L965 Earth Science Show
- A: Riko's poster and "Nobody interrupted Riko today!" work well. The first lantern window lights, as the test needs.
- B: The facts are right (a few cm a year, about the speed fingernails grow; four plates; about 111 active volcanoes). Where = place and so = result are fine. The JA in seg7 and seg19, "1ねんに ほんの すこしの センチメートル だけ", is unnatural.
- C: Reads well. "Snap a stick for rupt" is fine.
- Decision: seg7 and seg19 subtitleJa -> "1ねんに ほんの すうセンチ だけ" (objective JA).

#### L966 Why Do Earthquakes Happen?
- A: The two blocks with a sponge are a good demo. The panel and the question say "fix tall furniture to the wall". To a US child, fix = repair.
- B: The mechanism (push builds, rocks break and slip) and the safety advice (protect your head, stay away from windows and tall shelves) are right. The -ance spellings (enter -> entrance drops the e of er, guide -> guidance drops the e) are right. "Get under the table" comes before "hold on" (L976), as the order needs. The "fix" flag is the same as A's.
- C: TTS misreads the bare "ance" in seg9 ("Add ance") and seg10 ("Keep ance short and soft").
- Decision: teacherScript "fix ... to the wall" -> "attach ... to the wall" twice (A+B). seg9 -> "Add a, n, c, e" (B+C). seg10 -> "Keep the ending short and soft." with JA おわりは (B+C).

#### L967 Typhoons and Heavy Rain
- A: The quiet Watch Center and the swirl on the screen are natural. "I love water, but I never go near the river in heavy rain!" is a good Ember line.
- B: The weather card says only "The center of a typhoon is called the eye. It is calm there." That is true but unsafe on its own (the storm comes back), and the TPR also says "Stand still in the calm eye". In seg9, "Words with ent can end with ence ... sounds like ance" uses letter groups as words.
- C: In seg9, TTS will misread "ent", "ence" and "ance".
- Decision: teacherScript card -> "It is calm there, but the storm comes back soon." (objective fact; the seg5 definition and the TPR are kept). seg9 -> "Words that end in e, n, t can end in e, n, c, e. ... It sounds just like the end of distance" with JA to match (B+C).

#### L968 Tsunami Warnings
- A: The green arrow signs, the ten-minute walk and the camphor tree are natural, and "Never go to the beach to watch the waves" is clear.
- B: The facts and safety are right (a series of waves, jet speed in deep ocean, go at once, stay until the warning is over, more than one wave). The imperative "If you hear ~, go ~" is taught correctly. "If, then what to do." in seg8 is clipped but fine (single flag, kept).
- C: In seg9, "Ency sounds like en see" is a bare group plus an invented respelling.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Listen to the ending. Emergency. ... The ending is spelled e, n, c, y." with JA おわりの「ンシー」を よく きいてね (B+C).

#### L969 Listen to the Announcement
- A: The drill announcement, "It said to close the windows and not to go near rivers or the sea" and "If you can't hear an announcement clearly, ask an adult nearby" are good. In seg6, "I practice." (from the block-wide swap) sounds like a habit, not what Ms. Emma is doing now.
- B: "said to + verb" (and "not to") is taught right. The -ty spellings are right (keep the e in safety; honest + y). In seg9, "Add ty" is a bare group.
- C: TTS reads "Add ty" as "tie". The chime is "Ding, dong" in say, but the starter has "Ding-dong!", and the brief prefers the hyphen.
- Decision: seg6 -> "I give a practice announcement." with JA わたしが れんしゅうの ほうそうを するよ (A+C). seg9 -> "Add t, y" (B+C). "Ding, dong" -> "Ding-dong" in seg2, seg6, seg18, conversation line 0, the seg6 slide and the teacherScript (objective, brief keeper form).

#### L970 Disaster Science Quiz
- A: Team Lava vs Team Magma, the buzzers and the tie are fun. In seg6, "I read. Question one." is a bare fragment from the block-wide swap. The teacherScript line "Everyone came today, so there was no absence." is not natural.
- B: The quiz answers all match earlier lessons (magma, warm ocean water, your head, old ash, more than one wave), and 119 for fire / ambulance in Japan is right. Should = advice is clear. In the story Dr. Mori is the quiz master, but in the conversation Ms. Emma reads. Both are fine in their own telling (single flag, kept). The same "no absence" flag as A.
- C: In seg6, "I read." followed by "Question one." makes a stop-start rhythm.
- Decision: seg6 -> "I read the first question. Question one." with JA わたしが 1もんめを よむよ (A+C). teacherScript -> "so there were no absences." (A+B).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Bare letter groups in say for the W33 noun endings and roots**: "ion", "ly", "ure", "ance", "ence", "ent", "ency", "ty" were all said alone. Expect the same in L971-974 ("proof" alone), L981-984 ("ject" alone, which TTS may read as "jekt" or spell out), and the reviews L975, L980, L985-990. Grep say for `\b(ion|ly|ure|ance|ence|ent|ency|ty|proof|ject)\b` and spell the letters or put them in a real word. "rupt" alone is pronounceable and I kept it.
- **Fragments from the block-wide "Ms. Emma practices / reads" -> "I practice / I read" swap**: L969 "I practice." and L970 "I read." needed an object. L966-968 are fine ("I say, ~"); check the W34 lessons (L1002, L1006-1010) for a bare "I practice." / "I read." / "I say calmly." right before a quote.
- **"Ding, dong" vs "Ding-dong"**: L969 said had the comma form. Other announcement lessons (L977, L980, L990, W34 gate calls) should use "Ding-dong" in say.
- **"fix ~ to the wall"**: if L971-975 (the home check / kit lessons) use "fix furniture", change it to "attach" / "secure".
- **Typhoon eye**: if the eye comes up again (L975, L986-990 reviews), add that the storm comes back.
- Kept on purpose: L961 "Rupt means break. Snap!"; L964 "It's sunny, so I go to the park."; L965 "Bravo!"; L968 "If, then what to do."; L970 Dr. Mori vs Ms. Emma reading the questions; all "Very, very" / "Well done!" / "Thumbs up" items; Birimo's feminine endings (よ / のよ) when he plays Dr. Mori; "Safety first, fun always!".

### Run 9 review notes: L971-980 (world 33, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 53 patches in 9 lessons; L979 has no change. There are no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words, so no test or src line is affected (I grepped tests/ and src/lib/curriculum/ for every old string I replaced). The grammar order is clean: no "make sure" before L972, "hold on" before L976, "as soon as" before L978 or "unless" before L979. One non-tested slip is fixed: L976 seg20 said "stay calm" a lesson before L977 teaches it (the test reads teacherScript, expressions, TPR, conversation and starters, not segments). Themes: (1) **Safety and story logic**: L973's model plan put the family meeting place at the beach on a tsunami island (L979 itself says "We will not go to the beach"); L976 had Haru say "Pretend it's still shaking. Can we go now?" (he contradicts himself) and Birimo "too big for the desk" (he is the small one; "All the desks are taken!" also leads into "What if there's no desk?"); L978's teacherScript gave Riko's check line to Haru. (2) **The -proof gesture cue "Shield with your arm."** (L971-975), which has no object; now "Hold up your arm like a shield." to match the JA うでで たてを つくって. (3) **Answers with no speaker** in retold dialogue (L977 seg7, L980 seg7) so the asker seemed to answer herself. (4) **TTS and wording**: bare "ance" (L975), "Proof means protects against, or strong against" (L971, not a sentence), "Leak means drips come out", "Fix it, so that" (wrong pause for the purpose clause), "Great order!", "younger children = smaller than you". (5) **JA consistency**: もり はかせ -> モリ博士, アイランド・スクール -> 島の学校, みんな、やあ！ -> やあ、みんな！. Facts checked and right: about 3 liters of water per person per day, keep at least three days; 119 for fire and ambulance; drop, cover, hold on, away from windows and tall shelves, stairs not elevators; shoes by the bed; fix tall furniture; don't go back in until a building check; don't go to the beach or river to look; おはしも = don't push, don't run, don't talk, don't go back (in the right order); lantern windows 3 (L975) and 4 (L980). Every new sentence has 13 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK; all touched lessons are 218-227 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L971 Our Emergency Kit
- A: Ember's checklist (3 liters a person a day, three days, radio, whistle, papers in a waterproof bag) is clear and natural. seg10 "Shield with your arm." is not something a teacher says. seg11 "Leak means drips come out." is awkward. seg20 now ends "Great job!" (block-wide fix) and seg21 starts "Great packing!".
- B: The list pattern A, B, and C is modeled well. teacherScript "Proof means protects against, or strong against." has no subject or object; it is not a sentence. seg7 retells Birimo's line as "Ember says"; Birimo is playing Ember, so it is fine (single flag, kept). seg9 "Don't forget means please remember!" is a fair child gloss (kept).
- C: "Shield with your arm" stops the reader (shield what?). "drips come out" is clumsy aloud. "Great job! ... Great packing!" repeats.
- Decision: seg10 -> "Hold up your arm like a shield." (A+B); teacherScript -> "Proof means it keeps something out, or it's strong against something." (objective, grammar); seg11 -> "Leak means water drips out." (A+C); seg21 "Great packing!" -> "Nice packing!" (A+C; JA じょうずに つめられたね fits).

#### L972 Make Sure You…
- A: The Safety House, "Let's be safety detectives!", shoes by the bed and "Case closed!" are natural and useful. tpr[1] "Push the tall shelf. Is it safe?" is a mime with かるく おす in the action; a real child should not push a tall shelf, but it is pretend (single flag, kept).
- B: Make sure = do it and check it is explained well, with the allowed JA hint. seg9 "Fix it, so that it won't fall over." puts a comma before purpose "so that" while the target has none; with a comma it reads as a result ("and so").
- C: The comma puts a pause in the wrong place. seg10 has the same "Shield with your arm."
- Decision: seg9 -> "Fix it so that it won't fall over." (B+C). seg10 -> "Hold up your arm like a shield." (A+B).

#### L973 A Family Meeting Place
- A: Hilltop Park, the camphor tree and "say the exact spot" are realistic family-plan advice. seg17 Your turn models "Our family meeting place is the beach." On a tsunami island the beach is the place not to go.
- B: The exact-spot idea is taught clearly. The beach plan contradicts L979's promise "We will not go to the beach or the river to look around" and the whole high-ground theme. seg9 "Shield with your arm." again.
- C: Reads well; "Huff, puff!" and "Sniff, sniff" are fun.
- Decision: seg17 (say, subtitleEn) "the beach" -> "the school", JA うみべ -> がっこう (objective, fact/safety; "By the red bench!" kept). seg9 -> "Hold up your arm like a shield." (A+B).

#### L974 Waterproof and Fireproof
- A: Dr. Mori's paper-bag vs plastic-bag test and "That one stays at home, Haru!" are natural. Rain boots vs sandals is a good Your turn.
- B: Which is better ~? ~, because ~ is clear. seg11 "Earthquake-proof is long, so add a little line" is a simplification (the hyphen is about a less common compound) but fine for a child (single flag, kept). seg6 subtitleJa writes もり はかせ; the conversation and the rest of world 33 say モリ博士 (the only outlier).
- C: Reads well. seg10 "Shield with your arm." again.
- Decision: seg6 subtitleJa もり はかせ -> モリ博士 (objective, JA). seg10 -> "Hold up your arm like a shield." (A+B).

#### L975 My Family Safety Plan
- A: Safety Plan Day, the poster and the third lantern window are warm. The plan text is a good model presentation. "Its appearance is brighter now!" is a little bookish but uses the word on purpose (single flag, kept). "Riko, please share your family safety plan." is an invitation (lint info, kept).
- B: If there's ~, we will ~ (first conditional) is used correctly; the reading puts "protect our heads... When the shaking stops, we will go to Hilltop Park", so the order is safe. 119 for fire and ambulance is right for Japan. seg10 says "Appear plus ance." while the teacherScript spells "a, n, c, e".
- C: TTS misreads bare "ance" (ants / ahns). "E, N, C, Y" in capitals is read as letters (good).
- Decision: seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Appear plus a, n, c, e." (B+C; JA appear ＋ ance kept as written text). seg9 -> "Hold up your arm like a shield." (A+B).

#### L976 Drop, Cover, Hold On
- A: The drill, the poster and "hold the leg of the desk tightly, so the desk stays over you" are clear. Haru's "Pretend it's still shaking. Can we go now?" makes no sense (he says it is shaking and then asks to leave).
- B: Same Haru flag; Ms. Emma's answer "Not yet... until the shaking stops" needs Haru to ask whether it is over. Birimo "I'm too big for the desk!" contradicts the cast (Birimo is the small one); "All the desks are taken!" leads straight into his question "What should we do if there's no desk?". seg20 "and stay calm" uses the L977 target a lesson early. seg0 JA アイランド・スクール is the only one; everywhere else 島の学校. The up-up-down list intonation is explained in a way a voice can perform ("Drop, up. Cover, up. And hold on, down.").
- C: The Haru line trips the reader. List intonation reads well.
- Decision: conversation[4], seg8 and its slide item -> "Is it over? Can we go now?" with JA おわった？ もう いって いい？ (A+B). conversation[2], seg7, its slide item and teacherScript -> "All the desks are taken!" / "All the desks are taken, so Birimo covers his head with his arms." with JA つくえが ぜんぶ ふさがってる！ (A+B). seg20 -> "Cover your head with your bag, and stay where you are." with JA その ばに いてね (A+B). seg0 JA -> 島の学校 (objective, JA).

#### L977 Stay Calm
- A: "Moving out of the building is like a slow parade", the pencil-case quiz and Birimo's quiet turtle are lovely. seg7 ends "Kiko asks, Why shouldn't we talk? Because we need to hear the teacher." with no speaker for the answer, so Kiko seems to answer herself.
- B: The four rules match おはしも in order (押さない・走らない・しゃべらない・戻らない), and "osahashimo" is introduced as a Japanese school word (kept). The or-question rule (up on A, down on B = please choose one) is right. "Never use the elevator. Use the stairs" is right.
- C: Same seg7 flag: the listener cannot tell who answers. Commas before "or" in say give TTS a good pause (kept).
- Decision: seg7 (say, subtitleEn) -> "... I say, Because you need to hear the teacher." with JA わたし「…あるからよ。」 (A+C; matches Ms. Emma's conversation line).

#### L978 As Soon As the Shaking Stops
- A: The six mixed-up cards and the "After the Shaking" text are clear and correct (helmet or hood, stairs not elevator, class line, teacher checks everyone). seg19 "Great order!" sounds like praise for a food order.
- B: As soon as + present ("As soon as the shaking stops") is right, and "carefully means we listen to the teacher and walk with our class" heads off the "run out right away" reading. The teacherScript says "Haru checks the order. So, first we hide..." but in the conversation and seg8 it is Riko. seg0 JA みんな、やあ！ is unnatural order.
- C: "Great order!" is odd aloud. Lists read well.
- Decision: teacherScript "Haru checks the order" -> "Riko checks the order" (objective, story). seg0 JA -> やあ、みんな！ (objective, JA). seg19 "Great order!" -> "You got the order right!" (A+C; JA じゅんばん ばっちり！ kept).

#### L979 Unless It's Safe
- A: The lunch box story, "Not now. It can wait." and Riko sharing a cracker are natural. The teacherScript has an Island School boy and the conversation has Haru forget a lunch box; seg0 says Haru looks worried. Two tellings, not a contradiction (single flag, kept).
- B: Unless = except if (no double negative) is right, with the allowed JA hint. The safety promise (stay with the class, building check, no broken glass, not to the beach or river) is right. "Do we use the stairs or the elevator? The answer is always the stairs!" is a fine safety aside.
- C: Reads well.
- Decision: no change.

#### L980 Evacuation Drill
- A: The four kids as drill hosts, the announcement and the fourth lantern window are good. The announcer's "Good morning, everyone." is story-internal (kept). seg7 ends "Then I ask, Is everyone here? Yes, everyone is here!" with no speaker for the answer. "read the steps on the loudspeaker" (over is more usual) is a single flag (kept).
- B: seg5 defines younger children as "Kids who are smaller than you." Younger is about age, not size; the JA 小さい 子 is fine in Japanese but the English teaches the wrong meaning. Steps (First, ~. As soon as ~, ~.) are reviewed well.
- C: Same seg7 flag. "Please listen carefully." is an announcement (lint please-command is fine here).
- Decision: seg7 (say, subtitleEn) -> "... Haru says, Yes, everyone is here!" with JA ハルくん「…」 (A+C). seg5 -> "Little kids, like first graders." with JA 1年生のような 小さい 子たち (A+B).

#### Block-wide notes
- **"Shield with your arm."** is the -proof gesture cue in L971-975 (fixed here). Check L980's -proof review and any later W33 review lessons (L985, L990) for the same line; use "Hold up your arm like a shield."
- **Retold dialogue where the answer has no speaker**: "X asks, Q? <answer>" (L977 seg7, L980 seg7). Grep say for `asks?, [^.?!]*\? [A-Z][^,]*(\.|!)$` patterns and other Talk segments in W33-34; add "I say," / "Haru says,".
- **Order-pinned forms in teacher segments**: the test does not read teacher.segments, so "stay calm" slipped into L976 seg20. Grep segments for make sure / hold on / stay calm / as soon as / unless / how can we help / rebuilt (W33) and the W34 list before their start lessons.
- **Bare letter groups for TTS**: L975 "Appear plus ance". Check reviews (L980, L985, L990) for "plus ance / ence / ency / ty / rupt / ject" and spell them as letters.
- **JA name outliers**: もり はかせ (one in world 33, fixed), アイランド・スクール (one, fixed). Grep other JA for アイランド and もり はかせ in L981-990.
- **Safety in model sentences**: any Your turn or quiz that suggests the beach, a river or the sea as a meeting place or a place to look, on this tsunami island.
- Kept on purpose: L971 seg7 "Ember says" for Birimo-as-Ember; L972 tpr "Push the tall shelf" (mime); L974 "add a little line" for the hyphen; L975 "Its appearance is brighter now!" and "Riko, please share ..."; L977 "osahashimo"; L979 boy vs Haru tellings; L980 "on the loudspeaker", "Good morning, everyone." in the announcement, "Please listen carefully."; all "…" in the L972 title and slide.

### Run 9 review notes: L981-990 (world 33, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 29 patches in 8 lessons; L981 and L985 have no change. There are no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words, so no test or src line is affected (I grepped tests/ for every old string). The grammar order is clean: "how can we help" starts in L981, "rebuilt" in L984, and no new text adds an order-pinned form ("stay calm", added to the L988 teacherScript, is allowed from L977). Story checks still hold (Grandma Tomo in L981, "made-up" in the L984 teacherScript, "lantern" in L985 and L990, Airport of Wings and the speed TPR in L990). Themes: (1) **Ms. Emma in the third person inside her own teacherScript**: L982 "Kiko and Ms. Emma carry the oranges" (next sentence "each of us") and L987 "Ms. Emma nods and says" (seg6 already "I tap the card"). (2) **A British praise the block-wide swap missed**: L982 conversation line 6 "Lovely!". (3) **Phonics wording that does not say what the word means, or cannot be performed**: L986 "Rupt means burst, so a volcano bursts out" (skips eruption), L984 "Rejection, a no answer", L983 "Inject, put something into.", L989 "Dustproof keeps out dust" (adjective as subject), L989 "Is it a drill, up, or a real alarm, down?" (cues inside the question). (4) **Small natural-English and consistency fixes**: L982 "Hello, next door!", L986 "A big paper", L987 "Let me check, right? helps you ...", L988 Birimo's promise differing between teacherScript and conversation, L990 "Next, ... Next is World thirty-four". Facts checked are right: water about 3 liters per person per day for at least three days, drop / cover / hold on, stay away from windows and tall shelves, stairs never elevators, tsunami = high ground (L989 quiz), volcanoes give hot springs, rich soil and new islands, plates move very slowly, ject = throw with the right prefixes, confident -> confidence, urgent -> urgency, emerge -> emergency, guide -> guidance (e dropped), safety keeps the e. Ember is a salamander (L961), so "a very big salamander in a yellow helmet" in L986 is right. Every new sentence has 13 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK; all touched lessons are 221-233 s with 100% English and every slide item is valid.

#### L981 Helping Each Other
- A: The volunteer center, Grandma Tomo's "Many hands make light work!" and the job board are warm and natural. "Hello, helpers!" is a good opening.
- B: How can we help? / We can + verb is explained clearly; safe lifting (partner, bent knees) is right. "Volunteer. A person who helps others because they want to." uses a relative clause; it is in level for Stage 4 (single flag, kept). project (verb, throw forward) vs project (noun, big plan) is handled in the teacherScript.
- C: "Ject! ... Project. Projector. Subject." reads fine; "ject" is regular for TTS. "Brr, it's cold!" and "Ugh!" are easy.
- Decision: no change.

#### L982 Kind Words for Neighbors
- A: The orange man on the bench and the Kind Words Card are natural; the safety line "Always go with an adult, and never go into a house alone" is good. seg3 "Hello, next door!" is not something an American says (you say "Hi, neighbor!"). Conversation line 6 "Lovely!" is British praise.
- B: "next door" as a name for a person teaches a wrong use of the vocab item. The teacherScript says "Kiko and Ms. Emma carry the oranges to the man's gate. He gives each of us an orange." Ms. Emma is narrating, so she should be "I". "After strong wind or a big shake" is child-friendly (single flag, kept).
- C: "Excuse me, may I interject?" reads well. "Lovely!" is fine to say, but it is the British praise the block replaced.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Kiko and I carry the oranges" (objective). Conversation line 6 "Lovely!" -> "Nice job!" with JA よく できたね！ (objective, US policy). seg3 -> "Knock, knock! Hi! I live next door!" with JA こんにちは！ となりに すんで いる ものです！ (A+B).

#### L983 Helping People Who Don't Speak Japanese
- A: The ferry, the Japanese-only drill announcement and the helper card ("Use short, easy words and speak slowly") are realistic. seg9 "Inject, put something into." sounds cut off.
- B: This way, please / Do you speak English? are the right first moves; the quiz (A Do you speak English? vs B Why are you here?) is good. The teacherScript has Riko guide the traveler and the conversation has Haru and Kiko; they are separate tellings (single flag, kept). Facts on eject (a volcano ejects ash and rocks) are right.
- C: "put something into." ends on a preposition and the listener waits for more. "A tiny pinch, and all done!" is gentle.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Inject, put something inside." (A+C; JA なかへ 入れる fits).

#### L984 Rebuilding Together
- A: Grandma Tomo's picture book on a rainy day is lovely, and "This is a made-up story ... But it shows something true about helping" is clear. seg9 "Rejection, a no answer." is not natural English.
- B: The passive "were rebuilt by" is built well ("The houses didn't do it. The volunteers did it!", "Turn it around"); seg17 "The park was cleaned by volunteers" quietly shows was vs were. Everyone was safe because they went early (good safety message). "Pour some warm tea. Careful, it's hot!" is a tiny mismatch (single flag, kept).
- C: "Rejection, a no answer" is awkward to stress. "Were, rebuilt, by!" reads fine.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Rejection, when someone says no." (A+C; matches the teacherScript; JA ことわられる こと fits).

#### L985 Helper Role-Play
- A: Three stations, Birimo's big hat and the station cards are natural. The fifth window lights up, and "only one is still dark" fits L990.
- B: The review is clear; list intonation is explained as "Pack socks, up. Gloves, up. And a warm hat, down." "reject means say no" (seg9) vs "to say no" (teacherScript) are both fine. Kiko helps the traveler in both tellings.
- C: The arrows are explained in words a voice can perform. "Station one, station two, station three!" is fine.
- Decision: no change.

#### L986 A Safety Poster
- A: The poster scene and Riko's poster text are natural; the facts are right (two ways out, waterproof bag, about three liters per person per day for at least three days, drop / cover / hold on, away from windows and tall shelves). seg2 "A big paper with words and pictures" is a Japanese-English habit.
- B: "paper" is uncountable (a big paper = an essay or a newspaper). seg9 "Rupt means burst, so a volcano bursts out." does not say what eruption means; the teacherScript says "an eruption is when a volcano bursts out". Make sure = don't forget is right.
- C: "Squeak, squeak!" and "Peel, and stick!" read well. seg9 jumps from the root to the volcano.
- Decision: seg2 (say, subtitleEn) -> "A big sheet of paper with words and pictures on a wall." (A+B). seg9 -> "Rupt means burst. An eruption is when a volcano bursts out." with JA eruption は かざんが ふきだす こと (A+B).

#### L987 Reading Safety Instructions
- A: The evacuation card on the inn door is realistic (stay calm and listen to the staff, arrows, stairs never the elevator, walk, don't push, meeting place). seg8 "Let me check, right? helps you make sure you understand." is hard to follow.
- B: First / Then / Let me check ..., right? is a good checking strategy. The teacherScript says "Ms. Emma nods and says" while seg6 says "I tap the card"; the narrator is Ms. Emma. "At the inn, staff help guests" (no article) is acceptable in US English (single flag, kept). seg10 squeezes "Injection. In means into." between the or-question and "Your turn! Near or far?" (single flag, kept).
- C: seg8 glues two cue words into one subject; the reader cannot tell where the quote ends.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "I nod and say, Read the instructions." (objective). seg8 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Start with Let me check, and end with right? That helps you make sure you understand." with JA Let me check で はじめて、さいごに right? を つけると (A+C).

#### L988 My Safety Promise
- A: The star promise cards and Haru's promise card are warm and natural ("I will put in new batteries if the old ones are weak").
- B: I promise to + verb and the quiz (don't forget the to) are clear; confident -> confidence ("change the t to c, e") is right. Story contradiction: the teacherScript says "Birimo promises to learn every exit at the festival!", but in the conversation and seg7 Birimo says "And I promise to stay calm and help my friends!" goalJa keeps ほんとうの.
- C: Reads well; "Pinky promise!" is fun.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Birimo promises to stay calm and help his friends!" (objective, story). Riko's "stay calm and help her little brother" stays.

#### L989 Island of Fire Review
- A: The goodbye tour (Volcano Watch Center, Hilltop Park, the hot springs) and Dr. Mori's card are natural, and the facts are right. seg9 "Dustproof keeps out dust and sand." is not a sentence a teacher would say.
- B: dustproof is an adjective; the teacherScript says "A dustproof radio keeps out dust and sand." seg10 "Is it a drill, up, or a real alarm, down?" puts the cues inside the question, so the model sentence is never heard whole. urgent -> urgency is right. The tsunami quiz (beach vs high ground) is a good safety check.
- C: seg10 cannot be read with the target intonation (the cue words break the rise and fall). "Bravo!" is fine.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Dustproof means it keeps out dust and sand." with JA と いう いみ (A+B). seg10 -> "Is it a drill or a real alarm? A drill, up. Or a real alarm, down." with JA in the L987 style (B+C).

#### L990 World 33 Grand Review
- A: The festival program and the lantern lighting the way to the airport are a nice finish; Airport of Wings, the sixth window and the speed TPR are there. seg20 "Next, go and talk with Riko! Next is World thirty-four" repeats Next.
- B: Know it, prepare for it, and help each other is a good three-part list for the up, up, down review; emerge -> emergency (drop the last e) is right. The 8-line conversation is allowed here.
- C: The double "Next" sounds like a slip. "World thirty-four" is written in words for TTS (good).
- Decision: seg20 (say, subtitleEn) -> "After that, it's World thirty-four, the Airport of Wings!" with JA その つぎは (A+C). "Next, go and talk with Riko!" stays for the partner check.

#### Block-wide notes
- **Ms. Emma in the third person inside teacherScript**: the block-wide fix covered say / subtitles, but teacherScript also has "Ms. Emma nods and says" (L987) and "Kiko and Ms. Emma carry" (L982). Grep teacherScript for `Ms\. Emma (says|asks|nods|smiles|carries|points|taps)` and `and Ms\. Emma ` in the other W33-34 groups.
- **"Lovely!" in conversation lines**: the praise swap hit teacher segments but missed Ms. Emma's conversation line in L982. Grep conversation.lines[].en for `Lovely|Brilliant|Fantastic!` across the block.
- **Intonation cues inside the model sentence**: L989 seg10 had "Is it a drill, up, or a real alarm, down?". Check the other or-question / list reviews (L976-980, L990) for cue words placed inside the sentence; say the sentence whole, then the cues.
- **Root review lines that do not name the word's meaning**: "Rupt means burst, so a volcano bursts out" (L986), "Rejection, a no answer" (L984), "Dustproof keeps out ..." (L989). Look for the same shortcut in L961-964 (rupt) and L971-974 (-proof) recaps.
- **"Next, go and talk with X! Next is World ..."**: may recur in L1020 ("Next is the Hall of Wisdom").
- Kept on purpose: "A person who ..." definitions (Stage 4 level); L983 Riko vs Haru/Kiko guiding in two tellings; L984 "warm tea ... it's hot"; L987 "staff help guests" and the injection line in seg10; L988 Riko's little brother; Ember's catchphrase; the "Good morning!" said by Grandma Tomo, Riko and the old man inside the L982 story.

### Run 9 review notes: L991-1000 (world 34, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 40 patches. Every lesson has at least one change, and no lesson was left as is. There are no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is clean: "do you know where" first shows up in L995 and is not used before it, and no new text adds an order-pinned form (would you mind, could you tell me, recommend, I'm afraid, instead...). Themes: (1) **Ms. Emma in the third person in the teacherScript**: the block-wide fix covered say and subtitles, but all 10 teacherScripts in this group still had her own narration as "Ms. Emma says / asks / answers / sees / smiles / supervises us / her pouch" while she also says "we" and "us". I changed these to I / my / me, following the W15-16 precedent ("Ms. Emma is pretending" -> "I'm pretending"). Skye's note (L995) and card (L1000) still name Ms. Emma; that is right because Skye wrote them. (2) **Story and fact errors**: L994 Birimo's water bottle goes through security, so it has to be empty. In L996 the captain says "Good afternoon" right after a 10:45 takeoff, and he says drinks will come "in a few minutes" after Kiko already has her water. L997 says a visa means "Yes, we saw you". In L998, "by the sea, so we need one more plane" gives no real reason. In L1000 the "best part of the airport" model answer is the window seat. (3) **Number-reading wording**: "two numbers at a time" (L991, L1000) and "Three numbers?" (L994) mean digits. This is the same wording fixed in L906. (4) **TTS traps**: "Time time!" (L992), "goes up on A, and down on B" (L993; TTS reads the article a), "Or means a person" (L997), and "Ding, dong" (L994; the block keeper prefers Ding-dong). (5) **US usage**: "look out of the window" -> "look out the window" (L996, L1000), and "have a lovely stay" -> "have a great stay" (L999). I checked the numbers and they agree across lessons. Check-in is 7:15-10:00, boarding 10:15, the gate closes 15 min before departure, and Flight 205 leaves at 10:45 from gate 23B. Seats are 14A/B/C with Birimo in 15A and Ms. Emma in 15B, on a 3-3 plane where A and F are windows. Flight 1120 leaves at 4:20 from gate 41C and lands at 6:05. Passport control and customs come at the first airport, and the domestic connection comes after them. Yawning or chewing for the ears is right. Every new sentence has 13 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All lessons are 216-227 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L991 The Airport of Wings
- A: The wing-shaped roof, Skye the albatross guide and the Wing Stamp Book note ("This is not a passport.") are warm and clear. seg12 "Now open your Wing Stamp Book, with two hands." sounds off; people say "with both hands", and L992 uses "Both hands". In the teacherScript Ms. Emma says "Ms. Emma smiles and holds up her travel pouch ... she says", though she narrates in first person.
- B: Flight-number reading (two oh five, eleven twenty; 0 = oh) is correct. seg9 says "We read flight numbers two numbers at a time", but those are digits, and 205 is read as two / oh five. The teacherScript itself says "in small parts". "Every corner means everywhere, near and far" is a good gloss.
- C: The numbers are written as words in say, which is good for TTS. The comma in "Book, with two hands" adds an odd pause. The teacherScript "Ms. Emma ... she says" sounds like two narrators.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "I smile and hold up my travel pouch. Your passports are safe in here, I say." (A+C). seg9 -> "We read flight numbers in two parts." (A+B; JA 2けたずつ kept). seg12 -> "Now open your Wing Stamp Book with both hands." (A+C).

#### L992 Checking In
- A: The check-in sign, "Bye-bye, bags! See you later!" and Birimo climbing onto the scale are fun and natural. The clerk and Ms. Emma say "Good morning" inside the story, which is fine. The teacherScript has "Ms. Emma steps up ... and says" and "Ms. Emma takes our passports out of her travel pouch".
- B: "I'd like is a polite way to say I want" is right. The fifteen / fifty stress rule (fif-TEEN vs FIF-ty) is right and well drilled. The times all agree: check-in opens 7:15, closes 10:00, and the plane leaves 10:45. seg2 "show your ticket" is a little old-fashioned but not wrong (single flag, kept).
- C: seg9 "Time time!" sounds like a stutter. The hyphen beat forms (fif-TEEN, FIF-ty) are good for the voice.
- Decision: teacherScript Ms. Emma -> I step up / I take ... my travel pouch (A+C). seg9 "Time time!" -> "Clock time!" (A+C; JA じこくの じかん kept).

#### L993 Window or Aisle?
- A: The boarding pass reading is realistic. It has the gate, boarding at 10:15, the gate closing fifteen minutes before departure, and A and F at the windows. Haru's "I want to see the clouds!" is natural. In seg1, "Pick one and say, please." sounds like Ms. Emma saying please.
- B: The seats agree on a 3-3 plane: Haru 14A window, Riko 14B middle, Kiko 14C aisle, Birimo 15A window, Ms. Emma 15B. The choice-question rule (up on the first choice, down on the last) is right, but seg8 states it as "up on A, and down on B" with bare letters. The teacherScript has "Ms. Emma asks" and "Ms. Emma will sit right behind the children". Who asks "Can we sit together?" differs: Ms. Emma in the teacherScript, Riko in the conversation. Each version is fine on its own (single flag, kept).
- C: TTS reads "goes up on A" as the article a (the same trap as L902 "Make A strong"). "Pass!" in the speed challenge could sound like "skip" (single flag, kept).
- Decision: teacherScript -> "I ask, Can we sit together?" and "I'll sit right behind Haru, Riko, and Kiko, in 15B." (A+C). seg1 -> "Pick one and add please." (A+B). seg8 -> "Your voice goes up on the first choice and down on the last one." with JA はじめの ほうで … さいごの ほうで (B+C).

#### L994 Gate 23B
- A: The waiting-area announcements and "Shh! Listen!" are lively. The teacherScript says "while Ms. Emma watches us" and "Ms. Emma says, Let's get our things." "Ding, dong!" is not the usual spelling; it is "ding-dong".
- B: "Boarding means getting on the plane" is a clear gloss. Fact: Birimo's metal water bottle "beeps", and the staff "gives it back". A bottle with water in it would not be allowed through security, so it should be empty. seg8 "Three numbers? Split them." is the same digits issue as L991 (JA already says 3けた). In seg16 the practice announcement has Flight 318 at gate 15, although the story had it at 41C. It is a made-up practice line (single flag, kept).
- C: With the comma, "Ding, dong" is read as two separate words with a pause. The block keeper prefers the hyphen in say.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "and I watch everyone go through", "Birimo's empty metal water bottle", "Ding-dong!", "I say, Let's get our things." (objective: fact plus first-person narration). seg3, seg7, seg11 "Ding, dong" -> "Ding-dong" (A+C). seg8 -> "Three digits? Split them." (A+B).

#### L995 At the Airport
- A: The two signs, the staff member in the yellow vest, and Skye's note and goodbye work well. "Do you know where ... is? ― Yes! Go up the stairs ..." answers the question properly. The teacherScript has "Ms. Emma sees an airport staff member".
- B: The embedded question has no inversion and is explained as "a softer way to say, Where is gate 23B?". The number review is correct. Skye's note says "stay close to Ms. Emma", which is right because Skye wrote it.
- C: "Excuse me, plus Do you know." reads fine. "four-TEEN A" is in the hyphen beat form.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "I see an airport staff member in a yellow vest." (A+C). No other change.

#### L996 On the Plane
- A: The seatbelt sign, "Try yawning or chewing" and the cart are natural. The captain's announcement says "look out of your window"; American English is "look out your window" (block policy).
- B: Fact and story: Flight 205 leaves at 10:45, and the captain speaks soon after takeoff, so "Good afternoon" is wrong. Kiko asks for water and gets it from the cart before the captain says "In a few minutes, our flight attendants will serve drinks and a light lunch", so the order contradicts itself. Ears feeling strange and yawning or chewing to help are right. The vis gloss is right (vision, visibility, visual). The teacherScript has "Birimo and Ms. Emma" and "Ms. Emma says".
- C: Bare "vis" in say may be read as /vɪs/ or spelled out. It sits next to "V-I-S" and the real words, so it was kept (single flag).
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Birimo and I", "I say, Try yawning", captain "Hello, everyone.", "look out your window", "will serve a light lunch" with quiz answer "A light lunch.", "out the window" (objective: story, time and US policy).

#### L997 The Purpose of Your Visit
- A: The passport control sign ("children should stay with their adult") and the friendly officer are good. "Is this your first visit? Yes, it is!" is natural. seg9 "Visa. A paper that says, Yes, we saw you." is not something a visa says.
- B: Purpose, sightseeing, how long and where are right, and the airport order is right (passport control, then bags, then customs). seg8 "Or means a person." will be heard as the word or; the -or ending should be named. The core is pinned by a regex (no ― in "Sightseeing."), so it was kept. The teacherScript has three third-person Ms. Emma lines. goalJa keeps ほんとうの.
- C: "Or means a person" trips the listener. "Yes, we saw you. You may come in." sounds like a joke line.
- Decision: teacherScript Ms. Emma -> I (three places) (A+C). seg8 -> "The ending o, r means a person." with JA おわりの or (B+C). seg9 -> "Visa. A paper or stamp that says, We have seen your papers. You may come in." with JA to match (A+B).

#### L998 Transfer to Another Flight
- A: The departures board reading (on time / delayed / "check this board again") is realistic, and the transfer desk is clear. In the teacherScript, "Brightwater is a city by the sea, so we need to take one more plane" gives no reason; seg0 says "Brightwater is far away".
- B: Connecting flight and domestic flight are explained well. tele, in and super are right. The teacherScript "Ms. Emma supervises us. She watches over us" is third person, while seg9 already says "like me with you". tpr[3] and seg11 "Stay right next to Ms. Emma!" is a teacher's command to young children. American teachers often use their own name this way, and it is a TPR item, so it was kept (single flag). Flight 760 and gates 15 and 41C come back at a different airport with different destinations. That is possible (single flag, kept).
- C: Reads well. "Poof! Gone!" is fun.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "by the sea, far from here, so ...", "We all walk to the transfer desk, and I ask", "next to me", "I supervise you. I watch over you and keep you safe." (A+C).

#### L999 Changing Money
- A: The exchange counter sign, "Could we have some coins, too? For the bus!" and the seagull coin are nice. The sign ends "have a lovely stay!", which sounds British; American English would say "have a great stay!". Thirty dollars is a small amount for a family trip, but the children count only what they hold (single flag, kept).
- B: "Different countries use different money" and counting at the counter with your grown-up are good safety points. revise, advise and supervisor are right. The teacherScript has six third-person Ms. Emma lines ("The clerk gives Ms. Emma some bills. Ms. Emma asks ...").
- C: "Ms. Emma ... Ms. Emma ... Ms. Emma" back to back is heavy to read. "Zzzip!" is fine.
- Decision: teacherScript Ms. Emma -> I / me / my (six places) and "have a great stay!" (A+C).

#### L1000 Lesson 1000 Welcome!
- A: The view of the Clock Tower, the river and the castle, Skye with the golden 1,000 balloon, and her card make a happy celebration. The teacherScript says "Haru looks out of the window" while seg6 and tpr[0] say "look out the window". In seg16, "What was the best part of the airport? ... The best part was the window seat!" does not fit, because the window seat is on the plane.
- B: The times agree: Flight 1120 leaves at 4:20 and lands at 6:05. "We've arrived means we are here now, at last" is good. seg8 has "Two numbers at a time" again (digits). The teacherScript has "Ms. Emma says, One thousand lessons!". "1,000th" is written "one thousandth" in say, which is right for TTS. The second stamp is a golden balloon, and the test needs "stamp" and "thousand"; both are kept.
- C: "Today is a very, very special day" is warm for a celebration (keeper; single flag).
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Haru looks out the window." and "I say, One thousand lessons!" (objective: US policy plus first person). seg8 -> "Flight eleven twenty. In two parts." (A+B). seg16 -> "What was the best part of the trip?" with JA たびで (A+B; goalJa くうこうで kept).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Third-person Ms. Emma in the teacherScript**: the block-wide pass fixed say and subtitles only. In this group, all 10 teacherScripts still had "Ms. Emma says / asks / answers / sees / smiles / supervises us / her pouch". Please grep the teacherScript of all 60 lessons for `Ms\.
